[SYSTEM]
You are an RTL (Register Transfer Level) design agent. Your task is to create a Verilog/SystemVerilog design that satisfies the given specification, is functionally correct, and has minimal cost (area_delay_product).

**Cost metric `area_delay_product`:** area × achieved critical-path delay — the classic area-delay product. Does NOT include simulation cycle count (contrast with the 'area_runtime_product' metric). Cost is measured on your design alone. The testbench and everything it owns (checking logic, the external memories, etc.) is not synthesized and contributes nothing to area, delay, power, or energy.

## Specification
Design a module named fp_mul_e5f10 that multiplies two floating-point numbers. Format: 16-bit (1 sign + 5 exponent + 10 fraction). Inputs: a and b (16-bit each). Output: y (16-bit). Subnormal numbers must be supported.

Required behaviour, bit-exact: subnormal inputs are used at their true value; results are rounded to nearest, ties to even, including results in the subnormal range; a NaN input and inf x 0 give the canonical quiet NaN 16'h7E00; inf x finite gives inf with the product's sign; an overflowing product gives inf; a zero product gives a signed zero. The testbench enumerates about 18.9 million operand pairs against a reference implementation.

A working starting point is provided: `starting_point.v` in your workspace, a plain readable implementation (unpack, significand multiply, normalise, round, pack) that is correct. Optimize from there.

## Tools
- create_file: Create a new file in your working directory
- replace_file: Replace the entire contents of an existing file
- apply_diff: Apply a unified diff to modify a file
- edit_file: Edit a file by replacing an exact string match with new content. Provide old_string (must match exactly once) and new_string.
- ls: List files in your working directory
- read_file: Read the contents of a file
- run_evaluation(filename[, target_delay]): Run evaluation on the given design file. Returns correctness (Verilator simulation) + cost (Yosys area_delay_product). The filename must be your main design file (e.g. 'design.sv'). Optionally pass target_delay (ps) to override the synthesis timing constraint.

## Strategy
1. Lay out an action plan. Try to cover a diverse set of approaches in your plan to increase the chances of finding a good solution within the step limit. Also once you find a new best solution, explore close solutions. Trade off exploration with exploitation.
2. First, create a simple, straightforward design that is functionally correct. Focus on getting 100% correctness first.
3. Run evaluation to confirm correctness and get the initial cost (area_delay_product).
4. ONLY after achieving 100% correctness, try to optimize the design to reduce the area_delay_product.
5. After each optimization, run evaluation to verify correctness is maintained and check the new cost.
6. If an optimization breaks correctness, revert and try a different approach.
7. Keep iterating until you run out of steps — use every step to try improvements. The run ends automatically at the step limit; your best passing design is kept.

In general, be creative and take risk. At least after you have a correct design, try bold optimizations to reduce area_delay_product, even if they might break correctness. You can always revert if it doesn't work.
The best results you obtain which is 100% correctness counts.
Try to beat at least the straightforward implementation.

Running evaluation is crucial after every change to ensure correctness and track cost. Do not skip evaluations.

You may do some 'thinking' alongside with your tool calling responses to explain your reasoning, but always remember to call a tool in every response.

## Important
- Always call a tool in every response.
- You have a maximum of **60 steps** (LLM turns). Budget them wisely: get a correct design first, then optimise. You may batch tool calls in one response --- at most 8, and only one `run_evaluation`.
- The best result is the one with lowest cost among designs that pass 100% of testbench checks.
- Use different filenames for different design variants so you can retrieve them later.
- If context files (e.g. component source files) are in your workspace, you can modify them directly using edit_file or replace_file to optimize the design. Make targeted, incremental changes rather than rewriting files from scratch — small edits are less likely to introduce bugs.
- Write clean, synthesizable RTL code.
- You can pass `target_delay` (ps) to `run_evaluation` to try different synthesis timing constraints; experimenting with this may improve PPA results (lower target delay may reduce delay, higher target delay may reduce area/transistor count). target_delay must be > 0.
- Do not spend too many steps sweeping `target_delay` — basically if you want to minimize delay choose a low value,if you want to minimize area/transistor count choose a high value. That's it.A few targeted experiments are more productive than an exhaustive sweep.

## Delay Optimization Tips
- `target_delay` trades area against delay (a lower value pressures toward faster logic, a higher value lets Yosys re-map to smaller cells). Do not overly iterate on different target delays; often the RTL structure is the real lever, target_delay is only a minor secondary knob.
- After getting a correct design, try different accumulation orderings (sequential vs. tree) and compare results — Yosys synthesis can produce noticeably different delays (~10 ps) from structurally similar descriptions.
- For designs dominated by a multiply-accumulate chain, the critical path is the multiplier followed by the adder tree. Reducing the adder depth or splitting into parallel partial sums can help.

## Reference Implementations
The following are reference implementations for inspiration. Study them for patterns and techniques, but do not copy them directly — adapt ideas to your specific design.

### Parallel-prefix adder (ripple-carry / carry-lookahead) (Add.sv)
```systemverilog
// Copyright 2024 ETH Zurich and University of Bologna.
// Solderpad Hardware License, Version 0.51, see LICENSE for details.
// SPDX-License-Identifier: SHL-0.51
//
// Based on the work by Reto Zimmermann 1998 - ETH Zürich
// Originally written in VHDL, available under: 
// https://iis-people.ee.ethz.ch/~zimmi/arith_lib.html#library
//
// Authors:
// - Thomas Benz <tbenz@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Philippe Sauter <phsauter@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Paul Scheffler <paulsc@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
//
// Description :
// Binary adder using ripple-carry or parallel-prefix carry-lookahead logic.
// S = A+B

module Add #(
	parameter int              width = 8,             // word width
	parameter lau_pkg::speed_e speed = lau_pkg::FAST  // performance parameter
) (
	input  logic [width-1:0] A,  // operands
	input  logic [width-1:0] B,
	output logic [width-1:0] S   // sum
);

	// Function: Binary adder using parallel-prefix carry-lookahead logic.

	logic [width-1:0] GI, PI;  // prefix gen./prop. in
	logic [width-1:0] GO, PO;  // prefix gen./prop. out
	logic [width-1:0] PT;  // adder propagate temp

	// Internal signals for unsigned operands
	logic [width-1:0] Auns, Buns, Suns;

	// default ripple-carry adder as slow implementation
	if (speed == lau_pkg::SLOW) begin
		// type conversion: std_logic_vector -> unsigned
		assign Auns = A;
		assign Buns = B;

		// addition
		assign Suns = Auns + Buns;

		// type conversion: unsigned -> std_logic_vector
		assign S = Suns;
	end else begin
		// parallel-prefix adders as medium and fast implementations

		// calculate prefix input generate/propagate signals
		assign GI = A & B;
		assign PI = A | B;
		// calculate adder propagate signals (PT = A xor B)
		assign PT = ~GI & PI;

		// calculate prefix output generate/propagate signals
		PrefixAndOr #(
			.width(width),
			.speed(speed)
		) prefix (
			.GI(GI),
			.PI(PI),
			.GO(GO),
			.PO(PO)
		);

		// calculate sum bits
		assign S = PT ^ {GO[width-2:0], 1'b0};
	end
endmodule



module behavioural_Add #(
	parameter int              width = 8,             // word width
	parameter lau_pkg::speed_e speed = lau_pkg::FAST  // performance parameter
) (
	input  logic [width-1:0] A,  // operands
	input  logic [width-1:0] B,
	output logic [width-1:0] S   // sum
);
	assign S = A + B;
endmodule
```

### Prefix AND-OR structures (Sklansky, Brent-Kung, serial) (PrefixAndOr.sv)
```systemverilog
// Copyright 2024 ETH Zurich and University of Bologna.
// Solderpad Hardware License, Version 0.51, see LICENSE for details.
// SPDX-License-Identifier: SHL-0.51
//
// Based on the work by Reto Zimmermann 1998 - ETH Zürich
// Originally written in VHDL, available under: 
// https://iis-people.ee.ethz.ch/~zimmi/arith_lib.html#library
//
// Authors:
// - Thomas Benz <tbenz@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Philippe Sauter <phsauter@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Paul Scheffler <paulsc@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
//
// Description :
// Prefix structures of different depth (i.e. speed) for carry calculation
// in binary adders. Compute in m levels new generate/propagate signal pairs
// for always larger groups of bits. Generate signals of the last level correspond
// to carries in binary addition. Basic logic operations: AND-OR for generate
// signals, AND for propagate signals.

module PrefixAndOr #(
	parameter int              width = 8,             // word width
	parameter lau_pkg::speed_e speed = lau_pkg::FAST  // performance parameter
) (
	input  logic [width-1:0] GI,  // gen./prop. in
	input  logic [width-1:0] PI,  // gen./prop. in
	output logic [width-1:0] GO,  // gen./prop. out
	output logic [width-1:0] PO   // gen./prop. out
);

	// Constants
	localparam int n = width;  // prefix structure width
	localparam int m = $clog2(width);  // prefix structure depth

	// Sklansky parallel-prefix carry-lookahead structure
	if (speed == lau_pkg::FAST) begin : fastPrefix
		logic [(m+1)*n-1:0] GT, PT;  // gen./prop. temp
			assign GT[n-1:0] = GI;
			assign PT[n-1:0] = PI;
			for (genvar l = 1; l <= m; l++) begin : levels
				for (genvar k = 0; k < 2 ** (m - l); k++) begin : groups
					for (genvar i = 0; i < 2 ** (l - 1); i++) begin : bits
						// pass prop and gen to following nodes
						if ((k * 2 ** l + i) < n) begin : white
							assign GT[l*n+k*2**l+i] = GT[(l-1)*n+k*2**l+i];
							assign PT[l*n+k*2**l+i] = PT[(l-1)*n+k*2**l+i];
						end
						// calculate new propagate and generate
						if ((k * 2 ** l + 2 ** (l - 1) + i) < n) begin : black
							assign GT[l*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i] = 
												GT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i]
											  | (  PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i]
												 & GT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) - 1] );
							assign PT[l*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i] = 
													PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i]
												  & PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) - 1];
						end
					end
				end
			end
			assign GO = GT[(m+1)*n-1 : m*n];
			assign PO = PT[(m+1)*n-1 : m*n];
	end

	// Brent-Kung parallel-prefix carry-lookahead structure
	if (speed == lau_pkg::MEDIUM) begin : mediumPrefix
		logic [(2*m)*n -1:0] GT, PT;  // gen./prop. temp
		assign GT[n-1:0] = GI;
		assign PT[n-1:0] = PI;

		for (genvar l = 1; l <= m; l++) begin : levels1
			for (genvar k = 0; k < 2**(m-l); k++) begin : groups
				for (genvar i = 0; i < 2**l -1; i++) begin : bits
					if ((k* 2**l +i) < n) begin : white
						assign GT[l*n + k* 2**l +i] = GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l +i];
						assign PT[l*n + k* 2**l +i] = PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l +i];
					end // white
				end // bits
				if ((k* 2**l + 2**l -1) < n) begin : black
					assign GT[l*n + k* 2**l + 2**l -1] =
								GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l + 2**l - 1] |
							  | (  PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l + 2**l     -1] 
							     & GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l + 2**(l-1) -1]);
					assign PT[l*n + k* 2**l + 2**l -1] =
								PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**l     -1] 
							  & PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) -1];
				end // black
			end
		end // level1
		for (genvar l = m +1; l < 2*m; l++) begin : levels2
			for (genvar i = 0; i < 2**(2*m -l); i++) begin : bits
				if (i < n) begin : white
					assign GT[l*n +i] = GT[(l-1)*n +i];
					assign PT[l*n +i] = PT[(l-1)*n +i];
				end // white
			end // bits
			for (genvar k = 1; k < 2**(l-m); k++) begin : groups
				if (l < 2*m -1) begin : empty
					for (genvar i = 0; i < 2**(2*m -l -1) -1; i++) begin : bits
						if ((k* 2**(2*m -l) +i) < n) begin : white
							assign GT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
							assign PT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
						end // white
					end
				end // empty
				if ((k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l -1) -1) < n) begin : black
					assign GT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1] = 
								GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m-l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1]
							  | (  PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m-l) + 2**(2*m-l-1) -1] 
								 & GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m-l) -1] );
					assign PT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1] = 
								PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1]
							  & PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) -1];
				end // black
				for (genvar i = 2**(2*m -l-1); i < 2**(2*m -l); i++) begin : bits
					if ((k* 2**(2*m -l) +i) < n) begin : white
						assign GT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
						assign PT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
					end // white
				end
			end
		end // level2
		assign GO = GT[2*m*n -1 : (2*m -1) * n];
		assign PO = PT[2*m*n -1 : (2*m -1) * n];
	end  // Serial-prefix carry-lookahead structure
	else if (speed == lau_pkg::SLOW) begin : slowPrefix
		logic [n-1:0] GT, PT;  // gen./prop. temp
		assign GT[0] = GI[0];
		assign PT[0] = PI[0];
		
		for (genvar i = 1; i < n; i++) begin : bits
			assign GT[i] = GI[i] | (PI[i] & GT[i-1]);
			assign PT[i] = PI[i] & PT[i-1];
		end
		assign GO = GT;
		assign PO = PT;
	end

endmodule

```

[Step 1/60]
// fp_mul_e5f10 -- IEEE binary16 multiplier (1 sign, 5 exponent, 10 fraction bits) with subnormal support.

module fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);
  input  [15:0] a;
  input  [15:0] b;
  output [15:0] y;

  localparam BIAS         = 15;
  localparam MAX_E        = 31;   // inf / NaN
  localparam MAX_FINITE_E = 30;
  localparam PROD_W       = 22;   // 11 x 11 bit significand product

  // 1. unpack
  wire       s_a = a[15];
  wire       s_b = b[15];
  wire [4:0] e_a = a[14:10];
  wire [4:0] e_b = b[14:10];
  wire [9:0] f_a = a[9:0];
  wire [9:0] f_b = b[9:0];

  // 2. classify
  wire e_a_zero = (e_a == 5'd0);
  wire e_b_zero = (e_b == 5'd0);
  wire e_a_ones = (e_a == MAX_E);
  wire e_b_ones = (e_b == MAX_E);
  wire zero_a   = e_a_zero & (f_a == 10'd0);
  wire zero_b   = e_b_zero & (f_b == 10'd0);
  wire nan_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a != 10'd0);
  wire nan_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b != 10'd0);
  wire inf_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a == 10'd0);
  wire inf_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b == 10'd0);

  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);
  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;
  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;
  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;

  // 3. effective significands and exponents (subnormal: no hidden one, exponent 1)
  wire [10:0] m_a    = {~e_a_zero, f_a};
  wire [10:0] m_b    = {~e_b_zero, f_b};
  wire [4:0]  ea_eff = e_a_zero ? 5'd1 : e_a;
  wire [4:0]  eb_eff = e_b_zero ? 5'd1 : e_b;

  // 4. significand product, its leading-zero count (0 for a zero product), below[k] = |prod[k:0]
  wire [PROD_W-1:0] prod = m_a * m_b;

  reg [4:0] lz;
  integer   i;
  always @* begin
    lz = 5'd0;
    for (i = 0; i < PROD_W; i = i + 1)
      if (prod[i]) lz = 5'd21 - i[4:0];
  end

  wire [PROD_W-1:0] below;
  genvar k;
  generate
    for (k = 0; k < PROD_W; k = k + 1) begin : g_below
      assign below[k] = |prod[k:0];
    end
  endgenerate

  function bit_at;   // v[idx], 0 outside the vector
    input [PROD_W-1:0] v;
    input integer      idx;
    begin
      bit_at = (idx >= 0 && idx < PROD_W) ? v[idx] : 1'b0;
    end
  endfunction

  // 5. normalise and round a normal result: leading one onto bit 10
  wire              need_right = (lz <= 5'd11);
  wire [4:0]        sr         = 5'd11 - lz;   // right shift (wide product)
  wire [4:0]        sl         = lz - 5'd11;   // left shift (narrow product)
  wire [PROD_W-1:0] shifted    = need_right ? (prod >> sr) : (prod << sl);
  wire [10:0]       mant_pre   = shifted[10:0];

  wire guard    = need_right ? bit_at(prod,  sr - 1) : 1'b0;   // first bit shifted out
  wire sticky   = need_right ? bit_at(below, sr - 2) : 1'b0;   // any bit below the guard
  wire round_up = guard & (sticky | mant_pre[0]);              // round to nearest, ties to even

  wire [11:0] mant_round = {1'b0, mant_pre} + {11'd0, round_up};
  wire        carry      = mant_round[11];   // rounded up to a new leading one
  wire [10:0] mant_post  = carry ? mant_round[11:1] : mant_round[10:0];
  wire [9:0]  frac_norm  = mant_post[9:0];

  // 6. exponent of a normal result
  wire [7:0] exp_sum     = {3'd0, ea_eff} + {3'd0, eb_eff};
  wire [7:0] lhs         = exp_sum + 8'd1 + {7'd0, carry};
  wire [7:0] limit_under = BIAS + {3'd0, lz};
  wire [7:0] limit_over  = BIAS + MAX_FINITE_E + {3'd0, lz};
  wire       underflow   = (lhs <= limit_under);   // subnormal (or zero) result
  wire       overflow    = (lhs >  limit_over);    // past the largest finite number
  wire [7:0] e_norm      = lhs - limit_under;
  wire [4:0] exp_norm    = e_norm[4:0];

  // 7. subnormal result: shift the raw product onto the subnormal grid, round once
  integer           total_shift;
  reg [PROD_W-1:0]  sub_shifted;
  always @* begin
    total_shift = (BIAS + 10 + 1) - $signed({1'b0, exp_sum});
    sub_shifted = (total_shift >= 0 && total_shift < PROD_W) ? (prod >> total_shift) : {PROD_W{1'b0}};
  end
  wire [9:0]  frac_trunc = sub_shifted[9:0];
  wire        guard_s    = bit_at(prod,  total_shift - 1);
  wire        sticky_s   = bit_at(below, total_shift - 2);
  wire        round_s    = guard_s & (sticky_s | frac_trunc[0]);
  wire [10:0] frac_sum   = {1'b0, frac_trunc} + {10'd0, round_s};
  wire        carry_s    = frac_sum[10];   // rounded up to the smallest normal
  wire [9:0]  frac_sub   = frac_sum[9:0];
  wire [4:0]  exp_sub    = {4'd0, carry_s};

  // 8. pack
  wire is_nan      = nan_in;
  wire is_inf      = ~is_nan & (inf_in | (overflow & ~zero_in));
  wire is_sub      = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & underflow;
  wire sub_is_zero = is_sub & (exp_sub == 5'd0) & (frac_sub == 10'd0);
  wire is_zero     = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & (zero_in | sub_is_zero);

  wire [4:0] exp_y  = (is_nan | is_inf) ? 5'd31 : is_zero ? 5'd0 : is_sub ? exp_sub : exp_norm;
  wire [9:0] frac_y = is_nan ? 10'h200 : (is_inf | is_zero) ? 10'd0 : is_sub ? frac_sub : frac_norm;
  wire       sign_y = is_nan ? 1'b0 : s_y;

  assign y = {sign_y, exp_y, frac_y};
endmodule

`timescale 1ns/10ps
// exhaustive-directed tb (fpmul_run)
// Golden-referenced directed testbench: compares the DUT against the embedded
// golden reference (fp_mul_e5f10_ref, generated from context/starting_point.py)
// over ~18.9M enumerated vectors — no stimuli file. Structure:
//   1. the 3 known subnormal-boundary regression pairs (+ golden self-checks)
//   2. every subnormal/zero a (2^11 sign+mantissa patterns) x 4096 structured b
//   3. 4096 structured a x every subnormal/zero b
//   4. 2^21 LFSR-driven broad pairs
// Structured operands: both signs x all 32 exponents x 64 mantissas
// (8 corners + 56 LFSR-derived). The retired file-driven testbench is kept at
// tb_vectors.sv (+ vectors.dat) for cross-checking; it is not compiled.
module tb;
  logic [15:0] a, b, y, y_ref;
  int errors;
  longint total;

  fp_mul_e5f10 dut (.a(a), .b(b), .y(y));
  fp_mul_e5f10_ref refm (.a(a), .b(b), .y(y_ref));

  function automatic [15:0] lfsr_next(input [15:0] s);
    lfsr_next = (s >> 1) ^ (s[0] ? 16'hB400 : 16'h0000);
  endfunction

  logic [15:0] structured [0:4095];
  localparam logic [9:0] MANT_CORNERS [0:7] =
      '{10'h000, 10'h001, 10'h002, 10'h003, 10'h3FF, 10'h3FE, 10'h2AA, 10'h155};

  task automatic check();
    #1;
    total = total + 1;
    if (y !== y_ref) begin
      if (errors < 10)
        $display("TB_ERROR a=%0d b=%0d expected_y=%0d actual_y=%0d", a, b, y_ref, y);
      errors = errors + 1;
    end
  endtask

  logic [15:0] lfsr, ra, rb;
  logic [9:0] mant;
  int idx;

  initial begin
    errors = 0; total = 0;

    lfsr = 16'hACE1;
    idx = 0;
    for (int s = 0; s < 2; s++)
      for (int e = 0; e < 32; e++)
        for (int m = 0; m < 64; m++) begin
          if (m < 8) mant = MANT_CORNERS[m];
          else begin lfsr = lfsr_next(lfsr); mant = lfsr[9:0]; end
          structured[idx] = {s[0], e[4:0], mant};
          idx = idx + 1;
        end

    a = 16'h0003; b = 16'h6801; check();
    if (y_ref !== 16'h0E02) begin errors++; $display("TB_ERROR golden self-check 1"); end
    a = 16'h8003; b = 16'h6479; check();
    if (y_ref !== 16'h8AB6) begin errors++; $display("TB_ERROR golden self-check 2"); end
    a = 16'h7521; b = 16'h0003; check();
    if (y_ref !== 16'h1BB2) begin errors++; $display("TB_ERROR golden self-check 3"); end

    for (int sa = 0; sa < 2048; sa++) begin
      a = {sa[10], 5'b00000, sa[9:0]};
      for (int i = 0; i < 4096; i++) begin b = structured[i]; check(); end
    end
    for (int i = 0; i < 4096; i++) begin
      a = structured[i];
      for (int sb = 0; sb < 2048; sb++) begin b = {sb[10], 5'b00000, sb[9:0]}; check(); end
    end
    ra = 16'h0001; rb = 16'hBEEF;
    for (int k = 0; k < (1 << 21); k++) begin
      ra = lfsr_next(ra); rb = lfsr_next(lfsr_next(rb));
      a = ra; b = rb; check();
    end

    $display("TB_SUMMARY total=%0d errors=%0d", total, errors);
    if (errors != 0) $fatal(1, "FAIL");
    $display("PASS");
    $finish;
  end
endmodule

The testbench embeds a golden reference. Let me start by copying the starting point as my first design and evaluating it:
  -> create_file(filename='design.v', content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- IEEE binary16 multiplier (1 sign, 5 exponent, 10 fraction bits) with subnormal support.\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  localparam BIAS         = 15;\n  localparam MAX_E        = 31;   // inf / NaN\n  localparam MAX_FINITE_E = 30;\n  localparam PROD_W       = 22;   // 11 x 11 bit significand product\n\n  // 1. unpack\n  wire       s_a = a[15];\n  wire       s_b = b[15];\n  wire [4:0] e_a = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0] e_b = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0] f_a = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0] f_b = b[9:0];\n\n  // 2. classify\n  wire e_a_zero = (e_a == 5'd0);\n  wire e_b_zero = (e_b == 5'd0);\n  wire e_a_ones = (e_a == MAX_E);\n  wire e_b_ones = (e_b == MAX_E);\n  wire zero_a   = e_a_zero & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire zero_b   = e_b_zero & (f_b == 10'd0);\n  wire nan_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a != 10'd0);\n  wire nan_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b != 10'd0);\n  wire inf_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire inf_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b == 10'd0);\n\n  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;\n  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;\n\n  // 3. effective significands and exponents (subnormal: no hidden one, exponent 1)\n  wire [10:0] m_a    = {~e_a_zero, f_a};\n  wire [10:0] m_b    = {~e_b_zero, f_b};\n  wire [4:0]  ea_eff = e_a_zero ? 5'd1 : e_a;\n  wire [4:0]  eb_eff = e_b_zero ? 5'd1 : e_b;\n\n  // 4. significand product, its leading-zero count (0 for a zero product), below[k] = |prod[k:0]\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] prod = m_a * m_b;\n\n  reg [4:0] lz;\n  integer   i;\n  always @* begin\n    lz = 5'd0;\n    for (i = 0; i < PROD_W; i = i + 1)\n      if (prod[i]) lz = 5'd21 - i[4:0];\n  end\n\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] below;\n  genvar k;\n  generate\n    for (k = 0; k < PROD_W; k = k + 1) begin : g_below\n      assign below[k] = |prod[k:0];\n    end\n  endgenerate\n\n  function bit_at;   // v[idx], 0 outside the vector\n    input [PROD_W-1:0] v;\n    input integer      idx;\n    begin\n      bit_at = (idx >= 0 && idx < PROD_W) ? v[idx] : 1'b0;\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  // 5. normalise and round a normal result: leading one onto bit 10\n  wire              need_right = (lz <= 5'd11);\n  wire [4:0]        sr         = 5'd11 - lz;   // right shift (wide product)\n  wire [4:0]        sl         = lz - 5'd11;   // left shift (narrow product)\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] shifted    = need_right ? (prod >> sr) : (prod << sl);\n  wire [10:0]       mant_pre   = shifted[10:0];\n\n  wire guard    = need_right ? bit_at(prod,  sr - 1) : 1'b0;   // first bit shifted out\n  wire sticky   = need_right ? bit_at(below, sr - 2) : 1'b0;   // any bit below the guard\n  wire round_up = guard & (sticky | mant_pre[0]);              // round to nearest, ties to even\n\n  wire [11:0] mant_round = {1'b0, mant_pre} + {11'd0, round_up};\n  wire        carry      = mant_round[11];   // rounded up to a new leading one\n  wire [10:0] mant_post  = carry ? mant_round[11:1] : mant_round[10:0];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_norm  = mant_post[9:0];\n\n  // 6. exponent of a normal result\n  wire [7:0] exp_sum     = {3'd0, ea_eff} + {3'd0, eb_eff};\n  wire [7:0] lhs         = exp_sum + 8'd1 + {7'd0, carry};\n  wire [7:0] limit_under = BIAS + {3'd0, lz};\n  wire [7:0] limit_over  = BIAS + MAX_FINITE_E + {3'd0, lz};\n  wire       underflow   = (lhs <= limit_under);   // subnormal (or zero) result\n  wire       overflow    = (lhs >  limit_over);    // past the largest finite number\n  wire [7:0] e_norm      = lhs - limit_under;\n  wire [4:0] exp_norm    = e_norm[4:0];\n\n  // 7. subnormal result: shift the raw product onto the subnormal grid, round once\n  integer           total_shift;\n  reg [PROD_W-1:0]  sub_shifted;\n  always @* begin\n    total_shift = (BIAS + 10 + 1) - $signed({1'b0, exp_sum});\n    sub_shifted = (total_shift >= 0 && total_shift < PROD_W) ? (prod >> total_shift) : {PROD_W{1'b0}};\n  end\n  wire [9:0]  frac_trunc = sub_shifted[9:0];\n  wire        guard_s    = bit_at(prod,  total_shift - 1);\n  wire        sticky_s   = bit_at(below, total_shift - 2);\n  wire        round_s    = guard_s & (sticky_s | frac_trunc[0]);\n  wire [10:0] frac_sum   = {1'b0, frac_trunc} + {10'd0, round_s};\n  wire        carry_s    = frac_sum[10];   // rounded up to the smallest normal\n  wire [9:0]  frac_sub   = frac_sum[9:0];\n  wire [4:0]  exp_sub    = {4'd0, carry_s};\n\n  // 8. pack\n  wire is_nan      = nan_in;\n  wire is_inf      = ~is_nan & (inf_in | (overflow & ~zero_in));\n  wire is_sub      = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & underflow;\n  wire sub_is_zero = is_sub & (exp_sub == 5'd0) & (frac_sub == 10'd0);\n  wire is_zero     = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & (zero_in | sub_is_zero);\n\n  wire [4:0] exp_y  = (is_nan | is_inf) ? 5'd31 : is_zero ? 5'd0 : is_sub ? exp_sub : exp_norm;\n  wire [9:0] frac_y = is_nan ? 10'h200 : (is_inf | is_zero) ? 10'd0 : is_sub ? frac_sub : frac_norm;\n  wire       sign_y = is_nan ? 1'b0 : s_y;\n\n  assign y = {sign_y, exp_y, frac_y};\nendmodule\n")

[ASSISTANT]
Baseline passes with cost 145429. The critical path is very long (1338ps) going through the leading-zero counter, normalization shift, rounding, and packing. Let me now create an optimized version. The main issues:

1. The `always @*` with `for` loop for `lz` creates a priority encoder (expensive and slow)
2. The `bit_at` function with variable indexing creates MUXes
3. Two separate rounding paths (normal + subnormal)

Let me create a heavily optimized version:
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- optimized IEEE binary16 multiplier (1+5+10) with subnormal support.\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  localparam      BIAS = 15;\n  localparam [4:0] MAX_E = 31;\n  localparam [4:0] MAX_FE = 30;\n\n  // ── unpack ──\n  wire        s_a = a[15], s_b = b[15];\n  wire [4:0]  e_a = a[14:10], e_b = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0]  f_a = a[9:0], f_b = b[9:0];\n  wire        s_y = s_a ^ s_b;\n\n  // ── classify ──\n  wire ea_z = (e_a == 0), eb_z = (e_b == 0);\n  wire ea_o = (e_a == MAX_E), eb_o = (e_b == MAX_E);\n  wire fa_z = (f_a == 0), fb_z = (f_b == 0);\n  wire zero_a = ea_z & fa_z, zero_b = eb_z & fb_z;\n  wire inf_a = ea_o & fa_z, inf_b = eb_o & fb_z;\n  wire nan_a = ea_o & ~fa_z, nan_b = eb_o & ~fb_z;\n\n  wire nan_in = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;\n\n  // ── effective significands (11-bit, hidden=1 for normal, 0 for subnormal) & exponents ──\n  wire [10:0] m_a = {(~ea_z), f_a};\n  wire [10:0] m_b = {(~eb_z), f_b};\n  wire [4:0] ea_e = ea_z ? 5'd1 : e_a;\n  wire [4:0] eb_e = eb_z ? 5'd1 : e_b;\n\n  // ── 22-bit significand product ──\n  wire [21:0] prod = m_a * m_b;\n\n  // ── leading-zero count via priority OR chains ──\n  wire [21:0] orhi10 = prod >> 10;        // upper 12 bits\n  wire [21:0] orhi11 = prod >> 11;        // upper 11 bits\n  wire or_hi10 = |orhi10;  // any bit >= 10?\n  wire or_hi11 = |orhi11;  // any bit >= 11?\n  wire or_9    = |prod[9:0];\n  wire or_8    = |prod[8:0];\n  wire or_7    = |prod[7:0];\n  wire or_6    = |prod[6:0];\n  wire or_5    = |prod[5:0];\n  wire or_4    = |prod[4:0];\n\n  reg [4:0] lz;\n  always @* begin\n    if (or_hi11)      lz = 5'd21;\n    else if (or_hi10) lz = 5'd21;     // bit 10 set → lz=21 (but need 21 here means lz=21, product bit 10 is MSB)\n    else if (or_9)    lz = 5'd20;\n    else if (or_8)    lz = 5'd19;\n    else if (or_7)    lz = 5'd18;\n    else if (or_6)    lz = 5'd17;\n    else if (or_5)    lz = 5'd16;\n    else if (or_4)    lz = 5'd15;\n    else              lz = 5'd14;\n  end\n  // ── below[k] = |prod[k:0] ──\n  wire [21:0] below;\n  genvar k;\n  generate\n    for (k = 0; k < 22; k = k + 1) begin : g\n      assign below[k] = |prod[k:0];\n    end\n  endgenerate\n\n  // helper for indexed bits\n  function bit_at;\n    input [21:0] v; input integer idx;\n    begin\n      bit_at = (idx >= 0 && idx < 22) ? v[idx] : 1'b0;\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  // ── normalize: shift leading one to bit 10 ──\n  wire        need_r = (lz <= 5'd11);\n  wire [4:0]  sr = 5'd11 - lz;\n  wire [4:0]  sl = lz - 5'd11;\n  wire [21:0] shifted = need_r ? (prod >> sr) : (prod << sl);\n  wire [10:0] mant_pre = shifted[10:0];\n\n  wire guard = need_r ? bit_at(prod, sr - 1) : 1'b0;\n  wire sticky = need_r ? bit_at(below, sr - 2) : 1'b0;\n  wire round_up = guard & (sticky | mant_pre[0]);\n\n  wire [11:0] mant_round = {1'b0, mant_pre} + {11'd0, round_up};\n  wire        carry = mant_round[11];\n  wire [10:0] mant_post = carry ? mant_round[11:1] : mant_round[10:0];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_norm = mant_post[9:0];\n\n  // ── exponent of normal result ──\n  wire [7:0] exp_sum = {3'd0, ea_e} + {3'd0, eb_e};\n  wire [7:0] lhs = exp_sum + 8'd1 + {7'd0, carry};\n  wire [7:0] lim_u = 8'd15 + {3'd0, lz};\n  wire [7:0] lim_o = 8'd45 + {3'd0, lz};     // 15 + 30\n  wire       underflow = (lhs <= lim_u);\n  wire       overflow  = (lhs > lim_o);\n  wire [7:0] e_norm = lhs - lim_u;\n  wire [4:0] exp_norm = e_norm[4:0];\n\n  // ── subnormal result: shift product onto subnormal grid ──\n  integer total_shift;\n  reg [21:0] sub_shifted;\n  always @* begin\n    total_shift = (15 + 10 + 1) - $signed({1'b0, exp_sum});\n    sub_shifted = (total_shift >= 0 && total_shift < 22) ? (prod >> total_shift) : 22'd0;\n  end\n  wire [9:0]  frac_trunc = sub_shifted[9:0];\n  wire        guard_s = bit_at(prod, total_shift - 1);\n  wire        sticky_s = bit_at(below, total_shift - 2);\n  wire        round_s = guard_s & (sticky_s | frac_trunc[0]);\n  wire [10:0] frac_sum = {1'b0, frac_trunc} + {10'd0, round_s};\n  wire        carry_s = frac_sum[10];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_sub = frac_sum[9:0];\n  wire [4:0]  exp_sub = {4'd0, carry_s};\n\n  // ── pack ──\n  wire is_nan = nan_in;\n  wire is_inf = ~is_nan & (inf_in | (overflow & ~zero_in));\n  wire is_sub = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & underflow;\n  wire sub_zero = is_sub & (exp_sub == 0) & (frac_sub == 0);\n  wire is_zero = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & (zero_in | sub_zero);\n\n  wire [4:0] exp_y = (is_nan | is_inf) ? 5'd31 : is_zero ? 5'd0 : is_sub ? exp_sub : exp_norm;\n  wire [9:0] frac_y = is_nan ? 10'h200 : (is_inf | is_zero) ? 10'd0 : is_sub ? frac_sub : frac_norm;\n  wire       sign_y = is_nan ? 1'b0 : s_y;\n\n  assign y = {sign_y, exp_y, frac_y};\nendmodule\n", filename='design.v')

- If bit 10 is set: `lz = 11`, need_right, sr = 0  
- If bit 0 is set: `lz = 21`, need_right, sr = 0... wait sr = 11 - 21 which is negative in unsigned

Actually wait, let me re-read. The original does `lz = 21 - i[4:0]` for the first (lowest) set bit `i`. If the product is `prod = m_a * m_b` where both are 11-bit, the product is 22-bit. For two normal numbers, the leading bit is at position 21, so `i = 21` → `lz = 0`. Then `need_right = (0 ≤ 11) = true`, `sr = 11 - 0 = 11`, and `prod >> 11` puts bit 21 at bit 10. Correct.

For a subnormal × normal where leading bit might be at position 10, `i = 10` → `lz = 11`, `need_right`, `sr = 0`. Correct.

For a subnormal × subnormal, leading bit could be at position 0, `i = 0` → `lz = 21`, `need_right`, `sr = 11 - 21`. But sr is 5-bit unsigned, so `11 - 21` wraps to `5'b11100 = 28`... that can't be right. Actually, wait - let me re-check: if lz=21, `need_right = (21 <= 11) = false`, so `sl = 21 - 11 = 10`, `prod << 10`. If bit 0 is set, `prod << 10` puts it at bit 10. That's correct!

Good, back to baseline. Now let me think about the optimization strategy. The key insight: the LZ logic `for (i = 0; i < 22; i++) if (prod[i]) lz = 21 - i` finds the *first* set bit from bit 0 upward, which gives the lowest set bit → `lz = 21 - lowest_set_bit_index`.

Let me understand the LZ values:
- If `prod[21]` is set (and all lower are 0), `lz = 0` — but wait, the loop finds the *lowest* set bit. So if bit 21 is set and bit 0 is also set, `lz = 21`.

Actually no — the loop goes `i = 0, 1, ..., 21` and `if (prod[i]) lz = 21 - i`. The last assignment wins, so `lz = 21 - i` for the *highest* set `i`. So `lz = 21 - highest_set_bit`.

- Bit 21 set → `lz = 0`, need_right (0 ≤ 11), sr = 11, `prod >> 11` puts bit 21 at bit 10 ✓
- Bit 10 set → `lz = 11`, need_right, sr = 0 ✓
- Bit 0 set → `lz = 21`, NOT need_right (21 > 11), sl = 10, `prod << 10` puts bit 0 at bit 10 ✓

So `lz = 21 - MSB(prod)`. This is a leading-one finder from the top. Now let me create a proper priority encoder version:

[ASSISTANT]
Area went down (108.7 → 103.1) but delay went up. The area-delay product increased slightly. The priority encoder actually added delay. Let me try a different approach — since the product of two 11-bit significands where at least one has hidden bit=1 (normal) always has its MSB at bit 21 or 20, the LZ is usually 0 or 1. The complex LZ is only needed for subnormal inputs.

Let me think about this differently. The main cost drivers are:
1. The 11×11 multiplier (22-bit product) — this is unavoidable but dominates area
2. The barrel shifter for normalization 
3. The subnormal path (another barrel shifter + rounding)
4. The leading-zero counter

Let me try a fundamentally different approach: compute the product, then use a combined normalization+rounding+subnormal path. Also, let me try increasing target_delay to let Yosys use smaller cells:
  -> run_evaluation(filename='design.v', target_delay=2000)

[ASSISTANT]
Same result at 300. The synthesis tool seems to have a floor. The default 500 gives the same result. Now let me focus on structural RTL optimizations. 

Key observations from the critical path:
- Path starts at `a[10]` (exponent bit), goes through classification → product → normalization → rounding → packing
- The path is ~40 gates deep, which is very long

The biggest opportunity is to **simplify the design structure**. Let me try a radical rewrite that combines the normal and subnormal paths into a single unified shift+round path. The key insight: both paths do `prod >> shift_amount`, extract guard/sticky/round, and round. The difference is just the shift amount and the output format.

Let me also consider: for the common case (both normal), the product MSB is at bit 21, so `lz=0`, and the normalization shift is always `sr=11`. We can special-case this.
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- optimized IEEE binary16 multiplier (1+5+10) with subnormal support.\n// Unified normalize+round path to reduce area and delay.\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  localparam BIAS = 15;\n  localparam [4:0] MAX_E = 31;\n  localparam [4:0] MAX_FE = 30;\n\n  // ── unpack ──\n  wire        s_a = a[15], s_b = b[15];\n  wire [4:0]  e_a = a[14:10], e_b = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0]  f_a = a[9:0], f_b = b[9:0];\n  wire        s_y = s_a ^ s_b;\n\n  // ── classify ──\n  wire ea_z = (e_a == 0), ea_o = (e_a == MAX_E);\n  wire eb_z = (e_b == 0), eb_o = (e_b == MAX_E);\n  wire fa_z = (f_a == 0), fb_z = (f_b == 0);\n  wire zero_a = ea_z & fa_z, zero_b = eb_z & fb_z;\n  wire inf_a = ea_o & fa_z, inf_b = eb_o & fb_z;\n  wire nan_a = ea_o & ~fa_z, nan_b = eb_o & ~fb_z;\n\n  wire nan_in = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;\n\n  // ── effective significands & exponents ──\n  wire [10:0] m_a = {~ea_z, f_a};\n  wire [10:0] m_b = {~eb_z, f_b};\n  wire [4:0]  ea_e = ea_z ? 5'd1 : e_a;\n  wire [4:0]  eb_e = eb_z ? 5'd1 : e_b;\n\n  // ── 22-bit product ──\n  wire [21:0] prod = m_a * m_b;\n\n  // ── leading-zero count: lz = 21 - MSB_index(prod) ──\n  wire or21 = prod[21];\n  wire or20 = |prod[21:20];\n  wire or19 = |prod[21:19];\n  wire or18 = |prod[21:18];\n  wire or17 = |prod[21:17];\n  wire or16 = |prod[21:16];\n  wire or15 = |prod[21:15];\n  wire or14 = |prod[21:14];\n  wire or13 = |prod[21:13];\n  wire or12 = |prod[21:12];\n  wire or11 = |prod[21:11];\n  wire or10 = |prod[21:10];\n\n  reg [4:0] lz;\n  always @* begin\n    if      (or21) lz = 5'd0;\n    else if (or20) lz = 5'd1;\n    else if (or19) lz = 5'd2;\n    else if (or18) lz = 5'd3;\n    else if (or17) lz = 5'd4;\n    else if (or16) lz = 5'd5;\n    else if (or15) lz = 5'd6;\n    else if (or14) lz = 5'd7;\n    else if (or13) lz = 5'd8;\n    else if (or12) lz = 5'd9;\n    else if (or11) lz = 5'd10;\n    else if (or10) lz = 5'd11;\n    else           lz = 5'd12;\n  end\n\n  // ── below[k] = |prod[k:0] ──\n  wire [21:0] below;\n  genvar k;\n  generate\n    for (k = 0; k < 22; k = k + 1) begin : g\n      assign below[k] = |prod[k:0];\n    end\n  endgenerate\n\n  function bit_at;\n    input [21:0] v; input integer idx;\n    begin\n      bit_at = (idx >= 0 && idx < 22) ? v[idx] : 1'b0;\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  // ── normalize: shift leading one to bit 10 ──\n  wire        need_r = (lz <= 5'd11);\n  wire [4:0]  sr = 5'd11 - lz;\n  wire [4:0]  sl = lz - 5'd11;\n  wire [21:0] shifted = need_r ? (prod >> sr) : (prod << sl);\n  wire [10:0] mant_pre = shifted[10:0];\n\n  wire guard = need_r ? bit_at(prod, sr - 1) : 1'b0;\n  wire sticky = need_r ? bit_at(below, sr - 2) : 1'b0;\n  wire round_up = guard & (sticky | mant_pre[0]);\n\n  wire [11:0] mant_round = {1'b0, mant_pre} + {11'd0, round_up};\n  wire        carry = mant_round[11];\n  wire [10:0] mant_post = carry ? mant_round[11:1] : mant_round[10:0];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_norm = mant_post[9:0];\n\n  // ── exponent of normal result ──\n  wire [7:0] exp_sum = {3'd0, ea_e} + {3'd0, eb_e};\n  wire [7:0] lhs = exp_sum + 8'd1 + {7'd0, carry};\n  wire [7:0] lim_u = 8'd15 + {3'd0, lz};\n  wire [7:0] lim_o = 8'd45 + {3'd0, lz};\n  wire       underflow = (lhs <= lim_u);\n  wire       overflow  = (lhs > lim_o);\n  wire [7:0] e_norm = lhs - lim_u;\n  wire [4:0] exp_norm = e_norm[4:0];\n\n  // ── subnormal result ──\n  integer total_shift;\n  reg [21:0] sub_shifted;\n  always @* begin\n    total_shift = (15 + 10 + 1) - $signed({1'b0, exp_sum});\n    sub_shifted = (total_shift >= 0 && total_shift < 22) ? (prod >> total_shift) : 22'd0;\n  end\n  wire [9:0]  frac_trunc = sub_shifted[9:0];\n  wire        guard_s = bit_at(prod, total_shift - 1);\n  wire        sticky_s = bit_at(below, total_shift - 2);\n  wire        round_s = guard_s & (sticky_s | frac_trunc[0]);\n  wire [10:0] frac_sum = {1'b0, frac_trunc} + {10'd0, round_s};\n  wire        carry_s = frac_sum[10];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_sub = frac_sum[9:0];\n  wire [4:0]  exp_sub = {4'd0, carry_s};\n\n  // ── pack ──\n  wire is_nan = nan_in;\n  wire is_inf = ~is_nan & (inf_in | (overflow & ~zero_in));\n  wire is_sub = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & underflow;\n  wire sub_zero = is_sub & (exp_sub == 0) & (frac_sub == 0);\n  wire is_zero = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & (zero_in | sub_zero);\n\n  wire [4:0] exp_y = (is_nan | is_inf) ? 5'd31 : is_zero ? 5'd0 : is_sub ? exp_sub : exp_norm;\n  wire [9:0] frac_y = is_nan ? 10'h200 : (is_inf | is_zero) ? 10'd0 : is_sub ? frac_sub : frac_norm;\n  wire       sign_y = is_nan ? 1'b0 : s_y;\n\n  assign y = {sign_y, exp_y, frac_y};\nendmodule\n", filename='design3.v')

[ASSISTANT]
Same as design2. The priority encoder version is slightly worse. Let me think about what the original `for` loop synthesizes to — it's actually a priority encoder too, just expressed differently. The synthesis tool handles it well.

Let me try a completely different approach: instead of the LZ-based normalization, use a right-shift-only approach. The product of two 11-bit significands (with at least one normal) has its MSB at bit 20 or 21. The key insight is:

- Both normal: MSB at bit 21 (11+11-1), shift right by 11 → bit 10
- One normal, one subnormal: MSB at bit 20 (11+10-1), shift right by 10 → bit 10
- Both subnormal: MSB could be anywhere from bit 0 to bit 19

For the both-subnormal case, the result is always subnormal (or zero), so we can handle it entirely in the subnormal path. For the mixed/normal cases, the MSB is at bit 20 or 21, and we just need to detect which.

Let me try a design that splits into: fast normal path (handles normal×normal and normal×subnormal) and subnormal path (handles subnormal×subnormal, and all subnormal results):
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- optimized IEEE binary16 multiplier (1+5+10) with subnormal support.\n// Key optimization: for normal inputs, product MSB is at bit 20 or 21.\n// Only subnormal×subnormal needs full LZ.\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  localparam BIAS = 15;\n  localparam [4:0] MAX_E = 31;\n  localparam [4:0] MAX_FE = 30;\n\n  // ── unpack ──\n  wire        s_a = a[15], s_b = b[15];\n  wire [4:0]  e_a = a[14:10], e_b = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0]  f_a = a[9:0], f_b = b[9:0];\n  wire        s_y = s_a ^ s_b;\n\n  // ── classify ──\n  wire ea_z = (e_a == 0), ea_o = (e_a == MAX_E);\n  wire eb_z = (e_b == 0), eb_o = (e_b == MAX_E);\n  wire fa_z = (f_a == 0), fb_z = (f_b == 0);\n  wire zero_a = ea_z & fa_z, zero_b = eb_z & fb_z;\n  wire inf_a = ea_o & fa_z, inf_b = eb_o & fb_z;\n  wire nan_a = ea_o & ~fa_z, nan_b = eb_o & ~fb_z;\n\n  wire nan_in = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;\n\n  // ── effective significands & exponents ──\n  wire [10:0] m_a = {~ea_z, f_a};\n  wire [10:0] m_b = {~eb_z, f_b};\n  wire [4:0]  ea_e = ea_z ? 5'd1 : e_a;\n  wire [4:0]  eb_e = eb_z ? 5'd1 : e_b;\n\n  // ── 22-bit product ──\n  wire [21:0] prod = m_a * m_b;\n\n  // ── leading-zero count: lz = 21 - MSB_index(prod) ──\n  // Original approach: loop finds highest set bit\n  reg [4:0] lz;\n  integer   i;\n  always @* begin\n    lz = 5'd0;\n    for (i = 0; i < 22; i = i + 1)\n      if (prod[i]) lz = 5'd21 - i[4:0];\n  end\n\n  // ── below[k] = |prod[k:0] ──\n  wire [21:0] below;\n  genvar k;\n  generate\n    for (k = 0; k < 22; k = k + 1) begin : g\n      assign below[k] = |prod[k:0];\n    end\n  endgenerate\n\n  function bit_at;\n    input [21:0] v; input integer idx;\n    begin\n      bit_at = (idx >= 0 && idx < 22) ? v[idx] : 1'b0;\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  // ── normalize: shift leading one to bit 10 ──\n  wire        need_r = (lz <= 5'd11);\n  wire [4:0]  sr = 5'd11 - lz;\n  wire [4:0]  sl = lz - 5'd11;\n  wire [21:0] shifted = need_r ? (prod >> sr) : (prod << sl);\n  wire [10:0] mant_pre = shifted[10:0];\n\n  wire guard = need_r ? bit_at(prod, sr - 1) : 1'b0;\n  wire sticky = need_r ? bit_at(below, sr - 2) : 1'b0;\n  wire round_up = guard & (sticky | mant_pre[0]);\n\n  wire [11:0] mant_round = {1'b0, mant_pre} + {11'd0, round_up};\n  wire        carry = mant_round[11];\n  wire [10:0] mant_post = carry ? mant_round[11:1] : mant_round[10:0];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_norm = mant_post[9:0];\n\n  // ── exponent of normal result ──\n  wire [7:0] exp_sum = {3'd0, ea_e} + {3'd0, eb_e};\n  wire [7:0] lhs = exp_sum + 8'd1 + {7'd0, carry};\n  wire [7:0] lim_u = 8'd15 + {3'd0, lz};\n  wire [7:0] lim_o = 8'd45 + {3'd0, lz};\n  wire       underflow = (lhs <= lim_u);\n  wire       overflow  = (lhs > lim_o);\n  wire [7:0] e_norm = lhs - lim_u;\n  wire [4:0] exp_norm = e_norm[4:0];\n\n  // ── subnormal result ──\n  integer total_shift;\n  reg [21:0] sub_shifted;\n  always @* begin\n    total_shift = (15 + 10 + 1) - $signed({1'b0, exp_sum});\n    sub_shifted = (total_shift >= 0 && total_shift < 22) ? (prod >> total_shift) : 22'd0;\n  end\n  wire [9:0]  frac_trunc = sub_shifted[9:0];\n  wire        guard_s = bit_at(prod, total_shift - 1);\n  wire        sticky_s = bit_at(below, total_shift - 2);\n  wire        round_s = guard_s & (sticky_s | frac_trunc[0]);\n  wire [10:0] frac_sum = {1'b0, frac_trunc} + {10'd0, round_s};\n  wire        carry_s = frac_sum[10];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_sub = frac_sum[9:0];\n  wire [4:0]  exp_sub = {4'd0, carry_s};\n\n  // ── pack ──\n  wire is_nan = nan_in;\n  wire is_inf = ~is_nan & (inf_in | (overflow & ~zero_in));\n  wire is_sub = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & underflow;\n  wire sub_zero = is_sub & (exp_sub == 0) & (frac_sub == 0);\n  wire is_zero = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & (zero_in | sub_zero);\n\n  wire [4:0] exp_y = (is_nan | is_inf) ? 5'd31 : is_zero ? 5'd0 : is_sub ? exp_sub : exp_norm;\n  wire [9:0] frac_y = is_nan ? 10'h200 : (is_inf | is_zero) ? 10'd0 : is_sub ? frac_sub : frac_norm;\n  wire       sign_y = is_nan ? 1'b0 : s_y;\n\n  assign y = {sign_y, exp_y, frac_y};\nendmodule\n", filename='design6.v')

Let me think about this more carefully and try to unify everything into one shift amount:

The normal path shift is `sr = 11 - lz` (right shift to put MSB at bit 10). 
The subnormal path shift is `total_shift = 26 - exp_sum`.

For a normal×normal result (not subnormal), `lz = 0` (MSB at 21), `sr = 11`, and `exp_sum = ea_e + eb_e`. The normal result exponent is `exp_sum + 1 - 15 = exp_sum - 14`.

OK, I keep making the same design. Let me actually try a meaningful structural change. Let me compute sticky using a **priority-OR of shifted product** instead of the `below` prefix array. The idea: for the normal path with `sr` right shift, I can compute `sticky` by right-shifting `prod` by `sr-1` positions and OR-ing all bits below bit 0 (which is the guard).

Actually, a much simpler approach: compute `sticky = |(prod >> sr)` for all the bits that fall below the mantissa. When `prod >> sr`, the bits `[sr-1:0]` are lost. But I can compute a wider shift:

```
wire [24:0] wide = {3'b0, prod} >> sr;  // keep 2 extra bits
wire guard = wide[1];  // = prod[sr-1] mapped to bit 1
wire sticky = wide[0]; // = prod[sr-2] — but only if sr >= 2
```

Wait, this doesn't give me sticky as an OR of multiple bits. `wide[0]` = `prod[sr-2]` (just one bit). I need `|prod[sr-2:0]`.

The only way to get that from a shift is: `|(prod & ((1<<(sr-1))-1))` which is `|prod[sr-2:0]`. The `below` array IS the efficient way to compute this.

Let me try a totally different optimization: **reduce the multiplier from 11×11 to 10×10 + partial products**. The significand for a normal number is `1.xxxx` (11 bits), and for subnormal is `0.xxxx` (11 bits, but the hidden bit is 0). The product is 22 bits. 

Actually, for normal×normal: `m_a = {1, f_a}`, `m_b = {1, f_b}`. The product is `(256 + f_a) * (256 + f_b)` wait no, it's `(1024 + f_a) * (1024 + f_b)` since it's 11-bit with hidden 1 at bit 10. So `prod = (1024 + f_a) * (1024 + f_b) = 1024*1024 + 1024*f_a + 1024*f_b + f_a*f_b = 2^20 + 1024*(f_a + f_b) + f_a*f_b`.

This means the product always has bit 20 set (from `2^20`), and bit 21 is set if `f_a + f_b` causes a carry, or if `f_a*f_b` overflows. Actually `1024*1024 = 2^20` which is bit 20. So for two normals, the MSB is at bit 20 or 21.

If `f_a + f_b >= 1024`, then `1024*(f_a + f_b) >= 2^20`, which causes a carry to bit 21. Actually `1024 * 2047 = 2096128 = 2^21 - 2^11 + 2^20`... this is getting complicated. The point is, for two normals, MSB is at 20 or 21, so `lz` is 0 or 1.

For normal×subnormal: `m_a = {1, f_a}`, `m_b = {0, f_b}`. Product = `(1024 + f_a) * f_b`. MSB is at most bit 19 (since `1024 * 1023 < 2^20`), so `lz >= 2`.

For subnormal×subnormal: `m_a = {0, f_a}`, `m_b = {0, f_b}`. Product = `f_a * f_b` ≤ `1023 * 1023 < 2^20`. MSB at most bit 19.

So I could split the logic: if both normal, `lz` is 0 or 1 (determined by whether bit 21 is set). Otherwise, use the full LZ. But this doesn't reduce the multiplier.

Let me redo: `a = 2978`. `2978 / 32768 = 0` (sign 0). `(2978 >> 10) & 0x1F = 2` (exp=2). `2978 & 0x3FF = 2978 - 2048 = 930 = 0x3A2`. So exp=2 (normal), frac=0x3A2. Value = `2^(-13) * 1.9082... * 2^2 ≈ 0.000466`.

`b = 2247`. `(2247 >> 10) & 0x1F = 2` (exp=2). `2247 & 0x3FF = 199 = 0x0C7`. So exp=2 (normal), frac=0x0C7. Value ≈ `2^(-13) * 1.0483 * 2^2 ≈ 0.000257`.

Product ≈ `0.000466 * 0.000257 ≈ 0.00000012`. That's very small. `exp_sum = 2 + 2 = 4`. Normal path: `m_a = {1, 0x3A2} = 0x7A2 = 1954`, `m_b = {1, 0x0C7} = 0x4C7 = 1223`. `prod = 1954 * 1223 = 2389... let me compute: 1954 * 1223 = 1954 * 1000 + 1954 * 223 = 1954000 + 435742 = 2389742 = 0x247F6E`. That's 22 bits: `10 0100 0111 1111 0110 1110`. MSB at bit 21. `lz = 0`. 

`sr = 11`, `shifted = prod >> 11 = 2389742 >> 11 = 1166 = 0x48E`. `mant_pre = 0x48E`. `guard = prod[10] = (2389742 >> 10) & 1 = 2333 & 1 = 1`. `sticky = |prod[9:0] = |0x6E = 1`. `round_up = 1 & (1 | 0) = 1`. `mant_round = 0x48E + 1 = 0x48F`. `carry = 0`. `frac_norm = 0x48F`.

`lhs = 4 + 1 + 0 = 5`. `lim_u = 15 + 0 = 15`. `underflow = (5 <= 15) = true`. So subnormal.

`total_shift = 26 - 4 = 22`. `sub_shifted = (22 < 22)? no → 0`. But `total_shift = 22` which is NOT `< 22`, so `sub_shifted = 0`.

`guard_s = bit_at(prod, 21) = prod[21] = 1` (since MSB is at 21). `sticky_s = bit_at(below, 20) = below[20] = |prod[20:0] = 1`. `round_s = 1 & (1 | 0) = 1`. `frac_sub = 0 + 1 = 1`. `carry_s = 0`. `exp_sub = 0`. 

So the result should be exp=0, frac=1 → y=1. That matches expected!

So the bug in my design9 is that `total_shift = 22` is not `< 22`, so `sub_shifted = 0`. But `guard_s` and `sticky_s` need to be computed correctly. In my design9, `total_shift = 22`, and the `if (total_shift >= 1 && total_shift < 22)` is FALSE because 22 is not < 22. So `guard_s = 0` instead of `prod[21]`.

The fix: change `< 22` to `<= 22` for the guard/sticky, or handle the boundary case. Let me fix this:
  -> edit_file(filename='design9.v', old_string="    guard_s = 1'b0;\n    sticky_s = 1'b0;\n    if (total_shift >= 1 && total_shift < 22)\n      guard_s = prod[total_shift - 1];\n    if (total_shift >= 2 && total_shift < 22)\n      case (total_shift - 2)", new_string="    guard_s = 1'b0;\n    sticky_s = 1'b0;\n    if (total_shift >= 1 && total_shift <= 22)\n      guard_s = prod[total_shift - 1];\n    if (total_shift >= 2 && total_shift <= 22)\n      case (total_shift - 2)")

[ASSISTANT]
High target_delay → higher delay, slightly lower area but worse product. The default 500 is the sweet spot.

Now let me think about what structural changes could really help. The critical path is 1338ps through ~40 gates. The path goes through:
1. Classification (OR5, NAND, MAJ, XOR, XNOR chain) — ~350ps
2. Barrel shifter (O2A1O1I chain) — ~350ps  
3. Rounding add + exponent computation — ~350ps
4. Output MUX — ~300ps

Let me try a fundamentally different approach: instead of the two-path (normal + subnormal) structure, compute a single unified result. The key insight is that both paths are doing the same thing — shifting the product and rounding — just at different alignments.

Let me try computing the subnormal result from the normal result when it underflows. If the normal result has `exp_norm = 0` (subnormal), I can use the normal mantissa directly... but the normal path already normalizes the mantissa (leading 1 at bit 10), while subnormal results shouldn't have the leading 1.

Actually, let me try to merge the paths by using the subnormal shift for ALL results and deriving the normal result from it. The subnormal shift `total_shift = 26 - exp_sum` aligns the product so that the result exponent is `exp_sum - 14 - 1 = exp_sum - 15`. For a normal result (exp >= 1), the fraction is at bits [9:0] of `prod >> total_shift`... but wait, this doesn't account for the LZ normalization.

This is complex. Let me try a simpler structural change: try to make the `below` array smaller. Currently it has 22 entries (all prefix-ORs). But the normal path only uses `below[sr-2]` where `sr = 0..11`, so `sr-2` ranges from `-2` to `9` (only 0..9 are valid). The subnormal path uses `below[total_shift-2]` where `total_shift` ranges from ~0 to 22, so `total_shift-2` ranges from `-2` to `20`.

So I need `below[0]` through `below[20]` — 21 entries. That's essentially what I have. The `below` array is already fairly minimal.

Let me try a completely different approach: use a carry-save adder for the rounding instead of a full adder, or use a different rounding strategy that avoids the barrel shifter delay.

Actually, let me try the most impactful optimization I can think of: **share the barrel shifter**. Currently there are TWO barrel shifters — one for the normal path (`shifted = need_r ? prod >> sr : prod << sl`) and one for the subnormal path (`sub_shifted = prod >> total_shift`). If I merge them into one, I save area.

Actually, let me try a radically different approach to the whole problem. Instead of LZ-based normalization, let me compute the result directly using the exponent. The idea:

1. The product is `m_a * m_b` (22 bits)
2. The "raw" exponent is `ea_e + eb_e - 15` (biased)
3. For a normal result, the product MSB is at bit 20 or 21 (for normal×normal), and the normalized exponent accounts for this
4. For a subnormal result, shift right by extra bits

Let me try computing a unified shift amount:

The normal path shifts right by `sr = 11 - lz` to put MSB at bit 10, then the result exponent is `exp_sum + 1 + carry - 15 - lz`. 

The subnormal path shifts right by `total_shift = 26 - exp_sum` to align the binary point for subnormal representation.

For the subnormal case, `underflow` is true, meaning `exp_sum + 1 + carry <= 15 + lz`, i.e., `exp_sum <= 14 + lz - carry`. The subnormal shift is `26 - exp_sum >= 26 - (14 + lz) = 12 - lz`. And the normal shift is `11 - lz`. So the subnormal shift is always >= the normal shift + 1 (when underflow is true). This means the subnormal path shifts MORE than the normal path.

So for a unified approach: shift by `max(sr, total_shift)` and extract the result differently. But the extraction is different (11-bit vs 10-bit), so it's not trivially unifiable.

Let me try a different optimization: **simplify the subnormal path to avoid the second barrel shifter**. The subnormal path needs `prod >> total_shift` where `total_shift = 26 - exp_sum`. Instead of a full barrel shifter, I can derive the subnormal fraction from the normal path's shifted result. Since `total_shift >= sr + 1` (when underflow), I can do `shifted >> (total_shift - sr)` to get the subnormal fraction. This reuses the normal barrel shifter and adds a smaller right shift on top.

But `total_shift - sr = (26 - exp_sum) - (11 - lz) = 15 - exp_sum + lz`. For the subnormal case, this is `>= 1`. This is a smaller shift (at most 15+12=27 but typically small).

Actually, this adds complexity. Let me try another approach: **eliminate the LZ and barrel shifter entirely for the common case** and use a simpler structure.

For normal×normal (both have hidden 1), the product MSB is at bit 20 or 21:
- If `prod[21]` is set (overflow from multiplication): `lz=0`, shift right by 11
- If `prod[21]` is not set: `lz=1`, shift right by 10

I can compute this as: `shift_normal = prod[21] ? 11 : 10` and skip the full LZ. The full LZ is only needed when at least one input is subnormal.

Let me think about what the unified shift amount should be. For a normal result: shift right by `sr = 11 - lz`, mantissa at [10:0]. For a subnormal result: shift right by `total_shift = 26 - exp_sum`, fraction at [9:0].

When the result is subnormal, `total_shift > sr` (as computed above). So the unified shift = `max(sr, total_shift)`. When normal: shift = sr. When subnormal: shift = total_shift.

`total_shift = 26 - exp_sum`, `sr = 11 - lz`. `total_shift - sr = 15 - exp_sum + lz`. When `underflow` (exp_sum + 1 + carry <= 15 + lz), this is `15 - exp_sum + lz >= 1 + carry > 0`. So total_shift > sr when subnormal.

When normal (not underflow), `total_shift - sr = 15 - exp_sum + lz`. If this is <= 0, then sr >= total_shift, and we use sr. If > 0, we use total_shift (but this would be the subnormal case).

So unified_shift = `total_shift > sr ? total_shift : sr` = `(15 - exp_sum + lz > 0) ? total_shift : sr`.

But wait, this unified approach means I do one barrel shift by `unified_shift`, then:
- For normal: extract [10:0] (mantissa)
- For subnormal: extract [9:0] (fraction)

5'd1 : e_a;\n  wire [4:0]  eb_e = eb_z ? 5'd1 : e_b;\n\n  wire [21:0] prod = m_a * m_b;\n\n  // LZ: lz = 21 - MSB_index\n  reg [4:0] lz;\n  integer   i;\n  always @* begin\n    lz = 5'd0;\n    for (i = 0; i < 22; i = i + 1)\n      if (prod[i]) lz = 5'd21 - i[4:0];\n  end\n\n  wire [7:0] exp_sum = {3'd0, ea_e} + {3'd0, eb_e};\n\n  // Normal shift: sr = 11 - lz (right shift to put MSB at bit 10)\n  // Subnormal shift: total_shift = 26 - exp_sum\n  // Unified shift = max(sr, total_shift) but only right shifts are meaningful\n  // For lz > 11 (left shift case), we need to handle separately\n  \n  wire        need_r = (lz <= 5'd11);\n  wire [4:0]  sr = 5'd11 - lz;\n  wire [4:0]  sl = lz - 5'd11;\n  \n  // Subnormal shift (signed, can be negative = no shift needed)\n  wire signed [8:0] total_shift_s = 9'sd26 - $signed({1'b0, exp_sum});\n  wire [4:0]  total_shift = (total_shift_s >= 0 && total_shift_s < 22) ? total_shift_s[4:0] : 5'd0;\n  wire        ts_valid = (total_shift_s >= 0 && total_shift_s < 22);\n\n  // Unified shift amount: use total_shift when it's > sr (subnormal case), else sr\n  wire       use_sub_shift = need_r && ts_valid && (total_shift > sr);\n  wire [4:0] uni_shift = use_sub_shift ? total_shift : (need_r ? sr : 5'd0);\n\n  // Single barrel shift\n  wire [21:0] shifted_r = prod >> uni_shift;\n  wire [21:0] shifted_l = prod << sl;\n  wire [21:0] shifted  = need_r ? shifted_r : shifted_l;\n  wire [10:0] mant_pre = shifted[10:0];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_trunc = shifted[9:0];\n\n  // below[k] = |prod[k:0]\n  wire [21:0] below;\n  genvar k;\n  generate\n    for (k = 0; k < 22; k = k + 1) begin : g\n      assign below[k] = |prod[k:0];\n    end\n  endgenerate\n\n  function bit_at;\n    input [21:0] v; input integer idx;\n    begin\n      bit_at = (idx >= 0 && idx < 22) ? v[idx] : 1'b0;\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  // Unified guard/sticky\n  wire guard_u   = need_r ? bit_at(prod,  uni_shift - 1) : 1'b0;\n  wire sticky_u  = need_r ? bit_at(below, uni_shift - 2) : 1'b0;\n\n  // Normal rounding (mantissa at [10:0])\n  wire round_up_n = guard_u & (sticky_u | mant_pre[0]);\n  wire [11:0] mant_round = {1'b0, mant_pre} + {11'd0, round_up_n};\n  wire        carry = mant_round[11];\n  wire [10:0] mant_post = carry ? mant_round[11:1] : mant_round[10:0];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_norm = mant_post[9:0];\n\n  // Subnormal rounding (fraction at [9:0]) - same guard/sticky!\n  wire round_s = guard_u & (sticky_u | frac_trunc[0]);\n  wire [10:0] frac_sum = {1'b0, frac_trunc} + {10'd0, round_s};\n  wire        carry_s = frac_sum[10];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_sub = frac_sum[9:0];\n  wire [4:0]  exp_sub = {4'd0, carry_s};\n\n  // Exponent\n  wire [7:0] lhs = exp_sum + 8'd1 + {7'd0, carry};\n  wire [7:0] lim_u = 8'd15 + {3'd0, lz};\n  wire [7:0] lim_o = 8'd45 + {3'd0, lz};\n  wire       underflow = (lhs <= lim_u);\n  wire       overflow  = (lhs > lim_o);\n  wire [7:0] e_norm = lhs - lim_u;\n  wire [4:0] exp_norm = e_norm[4:0];\n\n  // Pack\n  wire is_nan = nan_in;\n  wire is_inf = ~is_nan & (inf_in | (overflow & ~zero_in));\n  wire is_sub = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & underflow;\n  wire sub_zero = is_sub & (exp_sub == 0) & (frac_sub == 0);\n  wire is_zero = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & (zero_in | sub_zero);\n\n  wire [4:0] exp_y = (is_nan | is_inf) ? 5'd31 : is_zero ? 5'd0 : is_sub ? exp_sub : exp_norm;\n  wire [9:0] frac_y = is_nan ? 10'h200 : (is_inf | is_zero) ? 10'd0 : is_sub ? frac_sub : frac_norm;\n  wire       sign_y = is_nan ? 1'b0 : s_y;\n\n  assign y = {sign_y, exp_y, frac_y};\nendmodule\n", filename='design12.v')

[ASSISTANT]
The unified approach has bugs. The problem is that when the result is subnormal, I use `uni_shift = total_shift`, but the normal path still rounds at bit 0 of the 11-bit mantissa, which is now misaligned because the shift is different from `sr`. Also, the subnormal fraction comes from a different bit position.

The core issue: when `use_sub_shift` is true (subnormal result), the normal path's mant_pre and rounding are wrong because the shift amount is different. The normal path computes `mant_pre = shifted[10:0]` using the subnormal shift, which puts the data in the wrong position.

I need to compute the normal result correctly even when the shift is the subnormal shift. Actually, when the result IS subnormal, the normal path result is not used (is_sub selects the subnormal result). But the `underflow` flag depends on `lhs` and `lim_u`, which depend on `carry` from the normal path. So the normal path needs to be correct to determine `underflow`.

The problem is: when I use the subnormal shift for the normal path, the `mant_pre`, `guard`, `sticky`, and `round_up` are all wrong, so `carry` is wrong, so `lhs` is wrong, so `underflow` might be wrong.

This unified approach is tricky. Let me abandon it and try something else.

Let me go back to the original design and try to optimize by **reducing the width of the `below` computation**. The `below` array has 22 entries, each a prefix-OR. But the normal path only uses indices 0-9 (sr-2 where sr=2..11). The subnormal path uses indices 0-20. So I need all 21 entries (below[0] through below[20]). Can't reduce this.

Let me try eliminating the `bit_at` function by using a different approach for the guard bit. The guard is `prod[shift-1]`. Since I already compute `below[k] = |prod[k:0]`, I can compute guard as `below[shift-1] & ~below[shift-2]` (i.e., bit shift-1 is set and nothing below it). But that's not right — guard is just `prod[shift-1]`, not the OR.

Actually, `prod[shift-1] = below[shift-1] & ~below[shift-2]` only if bit shift-1 is the ONLY set bit at or below shift-1. That's wrong. `prod[shift-1] = below[shift-1] ^ below[shift-2]` only works if there's exactly one bit difference... no, `below[shift-1] = |prod[shift-1:0]` and `below[shift-2] = |prod[shift-2:0]`, so `below[shift-1] & ~below[shift-2]` = `prod[shift-1]` (since the only new bit is `prod[shift-1]`). YES! `prod[k] = below[k] & ~below[k-1]` (for k > 0, and `prod[0] = below[0]`).

So `guard = bit_at(prod, shift-1) = bit_at(below, shift-1) & ~bit_at(below, shift-2)` (with appropriate out-of-range handling). This replaces the `prod` indexing with `below` indexing, but doesn't save much.

Let me try a completely different strategy. Instead of trying to micro-optimize, let me try to **pipeline the design with a register** to break the critical path. Wait, the testbench doesn't have a clock. Let me check if there's a clock...

Looking at the TB again: `module tb;` with `logic [15:0] a, b, y, y_ref;` and `fp_mul_e5f10 dut(.a(a), .b(b), .y(y));` — no clock port. The DUT is purely combinational. So I can't pipeline.

Let me try to reduce area by simplifying the special case handling. Currently the packing logic has a complex priority chain. Let me also try to merge the two rounding adders into one.

Actually, let me try a bold approach: **merge the two rounding paths into one**. Both paths compute `frac = trunc + round_bit` where `trunc` is 10 or 11 bits and `round_bit` is guard & (sticky | lsb). The normal path adds to an 11-bit mantissa, the subnormal to a 10-bit fraction. If I always compute the 11-bit version and then select bits [9:0] for subnormal, the carry bit might differ...

Actually, the subnormal path rounds at bit 0 of a 10-bit fraction (bits [9:0] of the shifted product), while the normal path rounds at bit 0 of an 11-bit mantissa (bits [10:0] of the shifted product). When the result is subnormal, the shift is larger, so the mantissa [10:0] includes an extra high bit that's not part of the subnormal fraction. The rounding for subnormal should be at bit 0 of the 10-bit fraction, not at bit 0 of the 11-bit mantissa.

When the unified shift is used (total_shift), `mant_pre = shifted[10:0]` includes bit 10 which is above the subnormal fraction. The guard for subnormal is `prod[shift-1]` = same as for the unified guard. But the LSB for ties-to-even is different: normal uses `mant_pre[0]`, subnormal uses `frac_trunc[0]` = `shifted[9:0][0]` = `shifted[0]` = `mant_pre[0]`. Wait, they're the same! `mant_pre[0] = shifted[0]` and `frac_trunc[0] = shifted[0]`. So the LSB is the same.

The difference is just the width: normal adds to 11 bits, subnormal adds to 10 bits. The carry from normal (bit 11) becomes `carry` (overflow to new leading 1), while carry from subnormal (bit 10) becomes `carry_s` (rounds up to smallest normal).

If I always compute the 12-bit sum `{1'b0, mant_pre} + round_up`, then:
- Normal carry = `sum[11]`
- Subnormal carry = `sum[10]` (since subnormal fraction is 10 bits)
- Normal frac = `sum[10:0]` (or adjusted for carry)
- Subnormal frac = `sum[9:0]`

This could work! Let me try:

Let me try to go back to the original and try a completely different optimization angle. The critical path has ~40 gates. The biggest contributor seems to be the barrel shifter and the exponent comparison chain. Let me look at what's actually on the critical path more carefully.

The path goes: `a[10]` → OR5 → NAND → XOR → XNOR chain → ... This is the classification → product → LZ → barrel shift → rounding → packing chain.

Let me try to reduce the depth of the barrel shifter. The barrel shifter shifts by 0-11 bits (right) or 1-10 bits (left). That's a 0-21 range. A barrel shifter for 22 bits with 0-21 shift has log2(22) ≈ 5 stages. But the actual shift amount depends on `lz` which comes from the priority encoder.

That's the same as the original again. Let me try a genuinely new approach: **compute the exponent comparison earlier** to determine if the result is subnormal before computing the full normalization. If the result is clearly normal (no underflow), skip the subnormal path. If clearly subnormal, skip the normal path.

The underflow condition is `lhs <= lim_u` where `lhs = exp_sum + 1 + carry` and `lim_u = 15 + lz`. Without `carry`, a preliminary check is `exp_sum + 1 <= 15 + lz`, i.e., `exp_sum <= 14 + lz`. For normal×normal (lz=0 or 1), this is `exp_sum <= 14` or `exp_sum <= 15`. Since `exp_sum = ea + eb` (both at least 1 for subnormal, at least 2 for smallest normal), the minimum exp_sum for two normals is `2+2=4`. So for small exponents, the result can be subnormal.

This doesn't help much. Let me try to reduce the area of the `below` array. The 22-entry prefix-OR array has significant area. What if I compute the sticky differently?

For the normal path, `sticky = below[sr-2] = |prod[sr-2:0]`. Since `sr = 11 - lz`, `sr-2 = 9 - lz`. For the common case `lz = 0`, `sr = 11`, `sticky = |prod[9:0]` — a 10-bit OR. For `lz = 1`, `sr = 10`, `sticky = |prod[8:0]` — 9-bit OR. These are simple.

For the subnormal path, `sticky = below[total_shift-2]`. `total_shift` can be up to 22, so this needs up to `below[20]` = 21-bit OR. But subnormal results are relatively rare. Can I compute the subnormal sticky only when needed?

Actually, the synthesis tool already optimizes this — the `below` array and `bit_at` MUXes are only on paths that are used. The area is what it is.

Let me try a completely different approach: **use a different rounding strategy**. Instead of guard/sticky/round, use "round to nearest even" by adding a bias before truncation. The approach: add `0.5 ULP` (with tie-breaking) to the product before truncation.

For the normal path, after shifting `prod >> sr`, I have mantissa at [10:0]. To round-to-nearest-even, I add `1` to bit -1 (guard position) if `sticky | lsb` is true. This is exactly what the current code does. No improvement here.

Let me try to **reduce the multiplier size**. The significands are 11 bits (1 hidden + 10 fraction). For normal×normal: `(1024 + f_a) * (1024 + f_b) = 2^20 + 1024*(f_a + f_b) + f_a*f_b`. I can compute this as `2^20 + 1024*(f_a + f_b) + f_a*f_b`, using a 10×10 multiplier (for `f_a*f_b`) and a 10-bit adder (for `f_a + f_b`) plus a 20-bit constant. This replaces the 11×11 multiplier with a 10×10 multiplier + adders.

A 10×10 multiplier is smaller than 11×11. Let me try this:

Verilator doesn't like the concatenation with shift. Let me use a different approach - just go back to the original `m_a * m_b` which works. Let me try a different optimization: simplify the `below` array. Instead of 22 prefix-ORs, I only need the ones that are actually indexed. The normal path indexes `below[sr-2]` where sr = 0..11, so indices -2 to 9 (only 0..9 are valid). The subnormal path indexes `below[total_shift-2]` where total_shift = 0..22, so indices -2 to 20. So I need `below[0]` through `below[20]` = 21 entries. That's what I have minus 1. Not a big saving.

Let me try to **eliminate the subnormal path's barrel shifter** by deriving the subnormal result from the normal result. The idea: when the result is subnormal, the normal path already computed `shifted` (product >> sr) and `mant_pre`. The subnormal result needs `prod >> total_shift` which is `shifted >> (total_shift - sr)` = `shifted >> (15 - exp_sum + lz)`.

Let me compute the subnormal fraction from the normal shifted result:
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- optimized: derive subnormal from normal shift, eliminate 2nd barrel shifter\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  localparam BIAS = 15;\n  localparam [4:0] MAX_E = 31;\n  localparam [4:0] MAX_FE = 30;\n\n  wire        s_a = a[15], s_b = b[15];\n  wire [4:0]  e_a = a[14:10], e_b = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0]  f_a = a[9:0], f_b = b[9:0];\n  wire        s_y = s_a ^ s_b;\n\n  wire ea_z = (e_a == 0), ea_o = (e_a == MAX_E);\n  wire eb_z = (e_b == 0), eb_o = (e_b == MAX_E);\n  wire fa_z = (f_a == 0), fb_z = (f_b == 0);\n  wire zero_a = ea_z & fa_z, zero_b = eb_z & fb_z;\n  wire inf_a = ea_o & fa_z, inf_b = eb_o & fb_z;\n  wire nan_a = ea_o & ~fa_z, nan_b = eb_o & ~fb_z;\n\n  wire nan_in = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;\n\n  wire [10:0] m_a = {~ea_z, f_a};\n  wire [10:0] m_b = {~eb_z, f_b};\n  wire [4:0]  ea_e = ea_z ? 5'd1 : e_a;\n  wire [4:0]  eb_e = eb_z ? 5'd1 : e_b;\n\n  wire [21:0] prod = m_a * m_b;\n\n  // LZ\n  reg [4:0] lz;\n  integer   i;\n  always @* begin\n    lz = 5'd0;\n    for (i = 0; i < 22; i = i + 1)\n      if (prod[i]) lz = 5'd21 - i[4:0];\n  end\n\n  wire [7:0] exp_sum = {3'd0, ea_e} + {3'd0, eb_e};\n\n  // Normal path\n  wire        need_r = (lz <= 5'd11);\n  wire [4:0]  sr = 5'd11 - lz;\n  wire [4:0]  sl = lz - 5'd11;\n  wire [21:0] shifted = need_r ? (prod >> sr) : (prod << sl);\n  wire [10:0] mant_pre = shifted[10:0];\n\n  // below array\n  wire [21:0] below;\n  genvar k;\n  generate\n    for (k = 0; k < 22; k = k + 1) begin : g\n      assign below[k] = |prod[k:0];\n    end\n  endgenerate\n\n  function bit_at;\n    input [21:0] v; input integer idx;\n    begin\n      bit_at = (idx >= 0 && idx < 22) ? v[idx] : 1'b0;\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  // Normal guard/sticky/round\n  wire guard    = need_r ? bit_at(prod,  sr - 1) : 1'b0;\n  wire sticky   = need_r ? bit_at(below, sr - 2) : 1'b0;\n  wire round_up = guard & (sticky | mant_pre[0]);\n  wire [11:0] mant_round = {1'b0, mant_pre} + {11'd0, round_up};\n  wire        carry = mant_round[11];\n  wire [10:0] mant_post = carry ? mant_round[11:1] : mant_round[10:0];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_norm = mant_post[9:0];\n\n  // Exponent\n  wire [7:0] lhs = exp_sum + 8'd1 + {7'd0, carry};\n  wire [7:0] lim_u = 8'd15 + {3'd0, lz};\n  wire [7:0] lim_o = 8'd45 + {3'd0, lz};\n  wire       underflow = (lhs <= lim_u);\n  wire       overflow  = (lhs > lim_o);\n  wire [7:0] e_norm = lhs - lim_u;\n  wire [4:0] exp_norm = e_norm[4:0];\n\n  // Subnormal path: shift prod by total_shift = 26 - exp_sum\n  // total_shift - sr = 15 - exp_sum + lz (the extra shift beyond normal)\n  // When underflow, this is > 0, so sub_shifted = shifted >> (15 - exp_sum + lz)\n  // But we still need guard/sticky from prod, which depend on total_shift\n  integer total_shift;\n  always @* begin\n    total_shift = (15 + 10 + 1) - $signed({1'b0, exp_sum});\n  end\n  // Compute sub_shifted directly from prod (keep original for correctness)\n  reg [21:0] sub_shifted;\n  always @* begin\n    sub_shifted = (total_shift >= 0 && total_shift < 22) ? (prod >> total_shift) : 22'd0;\n  end\n  wire [9:0]  frac_trunc = sub_shifted[9:0];\n  wire        guard_s = bit_at(prod, total_shift - 1);\n  wire        sticky_s = bit_at(below, total_shift - 2);\n  wire        round_s = guard_s & (sticky_s | frac_trunc[0]);\n  wire [10:0] frac_sum = {1'b0, frac_trunc} + {10'd0, round_s};\n  wire        carry_s = frac_sum[10];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_sub = frac_sum[9:0];\n  wire [4:0]  exp_sub = {4'd0, carry_s};\n\n  // Pack\n  wire is_nan = nan_in;\n  wire is_inf = ~is_nan & (inf_in | (overflow & ~zero_in));\n  wire is_sub = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & underflow;\n  wire sub_zero = is_sub & (exp_sub == 0) & (frac_sub == 0);\n  wire is_zero = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & (zero_in | sub_zero);\n\n  wire [4:0] exp_y = (is_nan | is_inf) ? 5'd31 : is_zero ? 5'd0 : is_sub ? exp_sub : exp_norm;\n  wire [9:0] frac_y = is_nan ? 10'h200 : (is_inf | is_zero) ? 10'd0 : is_sub ? frac_sub : frac_norm;\n  wire       sign_y = is_nan ? 1'b0 : s_y;\n\n  assign y = {sign_y, exp_y, frac_y};\nendmodule\n", filename='design16.v')

Let me try to reduce the **exponent comparison logic**. Currently:
- `lhs = exp_sum + 1 + carry` (8-bit add)
- `lim_u = 15 + lz` (8-bit add)
- `lim_o = 45 + lz` (8-bit add)
- `underflow = (lhs <= lim_u)` (8-bit comparison)
- `overflow = (lhs > lim_o)` (8-bit comparison)
- `e_norm = lhs - lim_u` (8-bit subtract)

Can I simplify? The underflow condition is `exp_sum + 1 + carry <= 15 + lz`, i.e., `exp_sum <= 14 + lz - carry`. The overflow condition is `exp_sum + 1 + carry > 45 + lz`, i.e., `exp_sum > 44 + lz - carry`. 

Since `lz` is 0-21 and `exp_sum` is 2-60, and `carry` is 0-1, these are 8-bit comparisons. Can I reduce the width? `exp_sum` max is 60 (30+30), so 6 bits. `lz` max is 21, so 5 bits. The comparisons need 6-7 bits. Currently using 8 bits which is slightly wider than needed.

Let me try using 6-bit arithmetic:

[ASSISTANT]
Very close - only 96 errors! The issue is `y=31744 = 0x7C00` which is inf with sign 0, and `y=64512 = 0xFC00` which is inf with sign 1. These are overflow cases where the result should be inf. The problem is that `exp_sum6` is only 6 bits, and `exp_sum6 + 1 + carry` could overflow. When `ea_e = 30` and `eb_e = 30`, `exp_sum6 = 60` (6 bits, fine). But `lhs6 = 60 + 1 = 61`, `lim_o6 = 45 + lz`. If `lz = 0`, `lim_o6 = 45`. `overflow = (61 > 45) = true`. That should work.

The issue is when `ea_e` and `eb_e` are both 31 (inf/nan). For inf inputs, `ea_o = 1` and the special case handling should catch it. But `ea_e` would be `e_a = 31` (since `ea_z = 0`), and `eb_e = 31`. So `exp_sum6 = 62` (6 bits, still fits). `lhs6 = 63`. `lim_o6 = 45`. `overflow = true`. But `is_inf` should be true (inf_in), so the overflow shouldn't matter.

Wait, the errors are for `a=1 b=1` → both are `0x0001` (subnormal with frac=1). `exp_sum6 = 1 + 1 = 2`. `lhs6 = 3`. `lim_u6 = 15 + lz`. Product = 1*1 = 1. MSB at bit 0. `lz = 21`. `lim_u6 = 15 + 21 = 36`. `underflow = (3 <= 36) = true`. So y should be 0, but getting 31744 = 0x7C00 (inf, exp=31, frac=0).

This means `is_inf` is being set when it shouldn't be. `is_inf = ~is_nan & (inf_in | (overflow & ~zero_in))`. For `a=1 b=1`: `inf_in = 0`, `zero_in = 0` (both subnormal with frac=1, not zero). `overflow` must be true! 

With 6-bit arithmetic: `lhs6 = exp_sum6 + 1 + carry = 2 + 1 + 0 = 3`. `lim_o6 = 45 + lz = 45 + 21 = 66`. But 66 doesn't fit in 6 bits! 66 = 0b1000010 which is 7 bits, so `lim_o6 = 66 mod 64 = 2`. So `overflow = (3 > 2) = true`! That's the bug — `lim_o6 = 45 + lz` can be up to 45+21=66 which overflows 6 bits.
